LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** Analyzing the spatial contradiction: The prosecutor (Franziska) states the crime scene is the P.I.C. meeting room on the 50th floor, and that the bloodstain in the meeting room suggests the body was moved after the stabbing. However, Evidence 11 (Elevator Blood) indicates blood was found on the secret elevator, suggesting the body was moved *using* the elevator. This creates a spatial conflict – the bloodstain implies movement *after* the stabbing, while the elevator blood suggests movement *during* the crime.

**Step 2:** Checking spatial propositions: Proposition 11 (The elevator blood suggests the culprit used the secret elevator to move the body) directly applies to this contradiction.

**Step 3:** Explicitly stating the proposition: Proposition 11 reveals the contradiction by presenting a scenario where the body was moved via the elevator, directly conflicting with the implication of the bloodstain in the meeting room being a post-mortem event. The elevator blood suggests a deliberate attempt to conceal the body’s location and movement.

**Step 4:** No further spatial reasoning is needed as the contradiction is clearly established by the conflicting evidence.

```json
{"evidence": 11, "testimony": 3}
```

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------

**Step 2:** Checking spatial propositions:

Prop 10: The bullet hole in the boarding lounge was created by a .38-caliber bullet. Prop 15: Evidence 15 states the bullet found near the victim’s oxygen tank was fired from a .38 caliber handgun.

**Step 3:** Explicitly stating the applicable propositions:

Prop 10 and Prop 15 both relate to the type of bullet involved in the incident. Prop 10 describes the bullet that created the hole in the lounge, while Prop 15 describes the bullet found near the victim’s oxygen tank. The contradiction lies in the assertion that two different bullets caused the same injury.

**Step 4:** Explanation of spatial reasoning:

The contradiction arises from the simultaneous assertion that the bullet hole was caused by a .38-caliber bullet and that a .38-caliber bullet was found near the victim’s oxygen tank. This suggests either the bullet hole was caused by a different bullet, or the bullet found near the oxygen tank was the same bullet that caused the injury.

Okay, let's analyze the provided information to find a contradiction and present it in the requested JSON format.

**Step 1: Analyze the spatial contradiction**

The core contradiction revolves around the Yatagarasu’s escape route and its location during the fire. Shih-na’s testimony states she heard a scream coming from the *next room over* while searching for the Yatagarasu. Simultaneously, Evidence 29 states the Yatagarasu escaped *through the revolving fireplace wall and into the next room over*. This suggests a simultaneous event – the Yatagarasu’s escape and a scream – occurring in the same location, which is inherently contradictory. Furthermore, Evidence 11 states the Yatagarasu was *in the middle of the room where the fire occurred*. This is a further complication, as the fire itself is a significant spatial element.

**Step 2: Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Prop 7: The Revolving fireplace wall in Babahl’s office was used by the Yatagarasu to escape.
Prop 11: The Yatagarasu was in the middle of the room where the fire occurred.

These two propositions directly relate to the identified contradiction.

**Step 3: If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 7 states the revolving fireplace wall was the escape route. Proposition 11 states the Yatagarasu was *in the room where the fire occurred*.  The combination of these two suggests the Yatagarasu used the fireplace wall to escape *from* the room where the fire was happening, which is a spatial inconsistency. It’s difficult to imagine a scenario where the Yatagarasu is simultaneously in the middle of a burning room and escaping through a wall within that same room. The scream reported by Shih-na could be related to the fire, but the simultaneous escape through the wall complicates the narrative.

**Step 4: If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The contradiction isn’t solely based on a single proposition. It’s a combination of the escape route (Prop 7) and the Yatagarasu’s location during the fire (Prop 11) that creates the conflict. The narrative suggests a single, contained space (the room with the fire) where the Yatagarasu both escaped and was present, which is logically improbable.
